## Request

# Central planning request

Prepare ACC v0.4.0 using the released ACC v0.3.4 to coordinate its own
development. This authorization covers phase 1 only: specification, architecture,
small tasks, per-task crew.toml contracts, import/status validation and a local
planning commit. Stop for owner approval before implementation or dispatch.

Use the approved central design: independent Claude Code/Codex coordinators in
Git worktrees; three authorized launch modes; a shared local registry; durable
file messages and questions; resumable headless and interactive sessions;
stdlib terminal and localhost web views; honest cost/rework/time/quality metrics;
explicit user command installation; optional Orca. Existing v0.3.x SDD is out
of implementation scope. List all eleven section-10 defaults for confirmation.

Write public English artifacts with Given/When/Then stories, FR/SC references,
spec-kit task grammar and a sidecar for every task. Each proof must use its own
deliverable and accepted prerequisites, never future tasks. Permit parallelism
only with non-overlapping files. Runtime is Python 3.11+ stdlib and Git on
Windows/Linux/macOS, with no Windows pty requirement. Preserve BOM/CRLF and
existing behavior. Tests must use fake CLIs and isolated temporary configuration,
never the owner's real configuration or subscriptions. No private data or
personal paths belong in public files, and no AI co-author trailer in commits.

Create project preferences manually with issue_tracker = "none", empty
sensitive_paths and Keep a Changelog. Do not install a second project skill,
run setup, edit global preferences, implement, merge, release or push. Explain
the proposed delivery split, task counts, decisions, risks and phase-2 entry
command in the approval handoff. This is a public scope transcription; local
machine paths from the operational request are intentionally excluded.

    Done. Final full-suite run: **25 passed, 1 skipped**.
    
    ## What I did
    
    Read only the two allowed files, then added ONE focused regression to `tests/test_central_messages.py`, placed next to the related equivalent-reply test:
    
    ```python
    def test_concurrent_equivalent_answers_share_one_id(tmp_path):
        root, cid = _root(tmp_path), _coord(tmp_path)
        qid = send(root, cid, _envelope(cid, direction="out", kind="question", task_refs=["T001"]))
        start = threading.Barrier(2)
    
        def contender():
            start.wait(timeout=5)
            return send(root, cid, _envelope(cid, kind="answer", reply_to=qid,
                                              task_refs=["T001"], body="choice"))
    
        with ThreadPoolExecutor(max_workers=2) as pool:
            first, second = pool.submit(contender), pool.submit(contender)
            ids = {first.result(timeout=5), second.result(timeout=5)}
        assert len(ids) == 1
        rows = pending(root, cid, "in")
        assert [row["id"] for row in rows] == [ids.pop()]
        assert questions(root) == []
    ```
    
    Design notes:
    - Two threads send **equivalent answers with fresh IDs** (the `_envelope` helper generates a new UUID per call) referencing the **same stored question**.
    - The barrier is **before** `send`, so it never blocks inside the protected write; the protected region stays purely serializing (no monkeypatching of `write_atomic`).
    - Asserts exactly what the brief asked: both sends return **one stable answer ID**, **one** pending answer remains, and the stored ID matches. Also confirms the question is correlated/closed.
    
    ## Result
    
    The test is **green on the first run** — the `_registry_lock`-protected check/dedup/write in `central_messages.send` correctly serializes the concurrent equivalent answers (first wins, second reuses its ID). No bug exposed, so no source fix was needed. I confirmed stability with 5 additional isolated runs (all `1 passed`). No other assertions or tests were modified.
    ```

    The proof command `python -m pytest tests/test_central_rounds.py -q` reports **10 passed**. The diff only adds the two allowed files (+1072 lines) and deletes no existing tests or code.
    
    ## Spec verdict
    - **FR-007: done.** Answers and questions go to the outbox only after the round file is saved. Only IDs that are both consumed and still pending get acknowledged, and an unknown ID rejects the whole round.
    - **FR-009: done, with one gap.** One round resumes one session, records the first session ID it sees and fails on a session mismatch. Logs are capped at 10 MiB and failures are kept. The gap is the DEADLINE problem below.
    - **FR-024: done.** Reads are bounded and symlinked or redirected `rounds/` and `logs/` folders are refused. Corrupt round files are skipped without being deleted.
    - **FR-025: done.** Usage numbers the CLI doesn't report are saved as null. The `estimated` flag only repeats what the adapter says; this file computes no character estimate. Recording usage once per event into the active job belongs to the T014 backfill. The schema is documented at lines 14-24.
    - **US2/AC1: done.** Nothing is acknowledged until the result is saved, so a retry delivers the message again.
    - **US2/AC5: done.** A failure only marks this one coordinator as failed and acknowledges nothing.
    - **extra:** none.
    
    ## Quality verdict
    - **Critical, central_rounds.py:377 and tests/test_central_rounds.py:99-110: the timeout does not cover writing the prompt.** The prompt is handed to the injected `spawn`, and the test's fake spawn writes all of it to the child's stdin before returning. If a child never reads a maximum-size prompt, that write blocks once the pipe buffer fills (about 64 KiB), before `wait(timeout)` at line 392 even starts. `run_round` should write stdin itself on a thread that the deadline covers. A test with a large prompt and a child that never reads is also missing.
    - **Important, central_rounds.py:397-398:** `drain.join()` has no timeout. A grandchild that escaped the kill and still holds a pipe open can hang the round forever. Joins should be bounded and the pipes closed.
    - **Important, central_rounds.py:153-155:** `glob` order is arbitrary, so once there are more than 256 round files the newest checkpoint can be silently skipped. Sorting by name or modification time first, or keeping a pointer to the latest checkpoint, would fix it.
    - **Minor, central_rounds.py:127:** `is_symlink()` doesn't catch Windows junctions on Python 3.11. The `resolve()` check that follows does catch them, but only after `mkdir`.
    - **Minor, central_rounds.py:9-11:** the docstring mentions a "default spawn" that doesn't exist.
    - **Minor, central_rounds.py:451-456:** an unusable session ID still overwrites `meta["session_id"]` with the requested one. That works, but it's easy to misread.
    - **Minor, tests:** there's no test where the first round captures a session ID with none requested, or where the adapter reports estimated usage.
    
    HEAD at end: `4644b0acc9d9949506eee48bb2588b3f83414454`

     terminal entry. `do_POST` always returns 405. There is also no `acc central --web` wiring. Some of this may belong to neighboring tasks, but the brief lists it under this task's criteria.
    - **FR-019: done.** The module imports only the stdlib and internal modules.
    - **FR-024: partial.** Traversal, encoded and double-encoded paths, null bytes, query strings, symlinks and over-long paths are all rejected. Reads are bounded: 1 MiB per asset, 64 KiB per string and 2048 items per collection. Isolation of corrupt records per coordinator is delegated to `central_snapshot` and not tested here.
    - **FR-026: done.** One `central_snapshot.snapshot()` call serves the API, and the module writes nothing. A read-only test compares the directory listing before and after. Parity with the terminal snapshot is only checked after sanitizing, which strips fields. The module is therefore a filtered projection, not an identical snapshot.
    - **SC-009: partial.** The tests cover parity, escaping (partly), traversal, loopback binding and cleanup, and use no live coordinators. They do not cover actions, because none exist. They do not cover Origin or token checks, because the code has none. The only check is on the Host header.
    - **US5/AC1: missing.** There is no browser UI, so no cards, pieces, charts, scoreboard or questions are rendered.
    - **US5/AC3: partial.**
      - Done: path traversal is blocked, and POST mutations are refused (405 for everything).
      - Not done: HTML escaping lives in the missing JS, so it is untested.
      - Not done: Origin and token checks for mutations do not exist.
    
    **Extra:** secret and path redaction with `briefcheck._SECRET_RULES`, security headers including CSP, a Host allowlist as DNS-rebinding defense, a snapshot lock, and port validation. These are reasonable, but none of them is named in a criterion.
    
    ### Quality findings
    - **Important** (lines 31-37, 41-50): the redaction blocklist drops any key containing "token". The `_PRIVATE_KEYS` set includes `token` and `request`, but metrics like `tokens_total` pass because only exact matches are removed. The `_public` substring filter covers password, secret, credential and api_key but not "token". The result is inconsistent and fragile. A key such as `auth_token` would leak.
    - **Important** (`_PATH`, line 21): the regex `(?<![\w:])/(?:...)` redacts any text containing `/`. That includes dates, "a/b" model names such as `gpt/4` and URLs, so legitimate message and job text gets corrupted.
    - **Important** (lines 41-64, `_public`): heavy sanitization with a redaction blocklist plus a top-level and row allowlist. This is more than any criterion asks for, and it will silently drop new snapshot fields. It couples this module to the private `briefcheck._SECRET_RULES`.
    - **Important:** `do_POST` is a stub that returns 405. FR-016 and US5 expect actions and controlled terminal entry. The test suite has no action tests (SC-009).
    - **Minor** (line 108): `self.server.previous = raw` keeps mutable server state. It is lock-protected, but it makes the handler stateful. `snapshot_lock` also serializes all snapshot requests, so a slow snapshot blocks every client.
    - **Minor** (`create_server`, line 138): it is annotated as returning `ThreadingHTTPServer` but returns `WebServer`. Attributes such as `root` and `assets` are set dynamically with no typing.
    - **Minor** (line 113): `except Exception` returns a generic 503 and drops the diagnostic. This is safe, but it means an operator cannot tell why the snapshot failed.
    - **Minor** (tests): the Host test hard-codes values, and the port-reuse test in `test_socket_released` can be flaky on some OSes. The symlink test skips on Windows without privileges.
    
    **Overall:** the transport and security skeleton is solid and well tested. The task does not meet its acceptance criteria as written, because there are no real assets, no UI, no actions and no Origin or token mechanism. I would send this back unless those parts were deliberately assigned to later tasks.
